At most 10,000 such records (`too_many_records`). A new identifier type adopting left-over numbers needs `confirm: [{ code: "key_adopts_values", key }]`.
  - *Signals:* a change's message counts every record of the organization it affects, on every site, and a template save's confirmations (`max_length_lowered` and the like) could be used to probe stored values by trying lengths. Both are moot now that only system managers, who see every record, save definitions (review item 3); if definitions are ever granted to a role again, these must be revisited.
- **The same gate for the other definitions (third review).** Vocabulary and record type saves (T-047) change public output too (a term's label, a record type's template), so they need this D-076 gate when they get their service operations. Field labels (and the template's label) are left out of the comparison because no public output shows them today; if a public page ever renders a `PublicView`'s field labels, label changes become public text and must count. `identifierTypeChanges` is called without its `existing` records on purpose, so every save of a public type asks for confirmation; the D-076 list comes from the database.
- **Hidden children (third review).** A unit's level or parent change reads its children's levels and its descendants' depth, and named a hidden child's level ("This unit holds a file now…"). For anyone but a system manager, a unit with children they can't view (filed on another site by a system manager) refuses a level or parent change with one fixed `in_use_below`, no level, count or kind, in the check, saves and restores alike. That reply differs from a unit with no children: a refusal has to say something, and allowing the change would break the arrangement for the hidden children. It is one more of D-074's accepted one-bit signals, like `in_use_below` for a change of site; two hidden children of different levels give byte-identical replies (tested).
- **Sign-in routes are not here.** Sign-in, the second step (T-038), password reset (T-039) and sign-out are T-045's routes beside these (credentials routes with the 8 KiB limit, cookie written through the reply's writer, D-069). The API depends only on `resolveSession`, so promotion and reset change nothing here.
- **Tests** (HTTP, `app.inject`, a fresh migrated database; full sessions through the real second step, T-038's `completeSecondStep` with a test recovery code): each endpoint with no session, a session waiting for its second step, an expired one and a made-up token (401, nothing written); each change with the CSRF header missing, wrong or another session's (403), from a foreign or no Origin (403), oversized (413) and with three wrong content types (415); the wrong role, the wrong working site (another site, another organization's, a made-up one), another organization's member; a missing record against another site's and another organization's record (identical status, headers and body, for read, update, delete and check); conflicts (412 with the names-only conflict, 428, 400); the dry run writing nothing, and answering a hidden new parent or represented record (B1) and a hidden unchanged parent under a level change exactly as missing ones; search per site, the cap, the timeout; site managers and catalogers refused definition saves; the D-076 lists for each review repro (a field made public, B2 a new inherited key, B3 a removed stopper key, B4 a published child of a number holder, B5 `maxLength`, a new option and an option's label); `publishedGate`'s `records.publish` line directly; the rate limit; the redacted log.
- **The build order.** `npm run build` names every workspace in dependency order (core, db, service, api), because npm's `--workspaces` order is alphabetical and would build `@oculus/api` first. `scripts/build-order.test.mjs` fails when a package with a build script is missing from the list or comes before a package it depends on, so the admin package (D-073) can't be skipped silently (review item 4).
- **Left for later:** scoped grants in search (above); history, restore, workflow and publishing endpoints (T-043); sign-in routes (T-045); serving the admin on its own hostname with its CSP (T-050); a list endpoint for records (T-046's Items list).
- Sources: RFC 9110 §13.1.1 (`If-Match`), §13.1.2 (`If-None-Match: *`), §15.5.13 and RFC 6585 §3 (`428 Precondition Required`), RFC 6585 §4 (`429`); OWASP ASVS 5.0 V3.5 (CSRF), V7 (sessions), V8 (authorization: deny by default, check at the trusted service layer, answer what may not be seen as if it didn't exist), V16 (no sensitive data in logs); OWASP Cross-Site Request Forgery Prevention Cheat Sheet (custom request headers with a session-bound token); PostgreSQL 16 `runtime-config-client.html` (`statement_timeout`, `SET LOCAL` via `set_config(…, true)`), as already cited in D-058, D-066 and D-069.

## D-080 — Public reads go by site, through one SECURITY DEFINER function, with a public reader role that can read no table (2026-11-24, manual session, S-019)
The 2026-11-24 security review found that `readPublicRecord(db, organizationId, recordId)` never looked at `records.site_id` (a record published on one site went out on every public site of the organization, records on no site too, and links resolved organization-wide), and that it needed a connection with SELECT on the tables it read, in practice on every table (its test used such a role), so a public site's connection could read every staff value, password hash, session and sealed second factor. D-074 gives items a home site; D-075 leaves per-site *showing* ("Show on these sites") to Phase 5. This is the interim rule and the read path. Migration `0017_public_reads.sql`, `packages/db/src/workflow.ts`, `roles.ts`.
- **The rule until Phase 5 and T-041b:** a record is public on site S when it is `published` **and S is its home site** (`public.record_public_on_site(r, s)`). A record on no site is never public (D-074: items on no site are only for system managers to place). `readPublicRecord(db, organizationId, siteId, recordId, options)` takes the public site; a record public on another site, on no site, unpublished, missing or another organization's, and a site that isn't the organization's, all get the identical `not_available` (tested as equal results). Links and places resolve only to records public on the same site; any other target goes out as nothing (D-045, D-048), exactly as an unpublished one did. **Hook for T-041b:** records accepted into S from another site (D-074's sharing) are added in `record_public_on_site` and nowhere else; Phase 5's per-site showing narrows the same function.
- **Inherited values** still follow D-016 (values inherited from ancestors may go out on a published child, never an unpublished ancestor's title or ID). An ancestor public on another site counts as not public here, so its title and ID don't go out on S. Ancestors on another site exist only where a system manager filed across sites (service `other_site` refuses it for everyone else); S-020 makes publishing check view permission on every ancestor whose values go out.
- **One read path, nothing else readable.** `public.public_record_snapshot(org, site, record)` (LANGUAGE sql, STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER owned by the migrating owner, `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, every name qualified) returns NULL unless the record is public on the site, and otherwise one jsonb value with exactly what the public output is computed from: the record's ancestry and represented records, the records their values link to (found as core's `linkedIds` finds them) with their ancestry, which of those are public on the site, the current version of each of their templates, the organization's identifier types, and the identifiers of the record's ancestry; or `tooMany` past 10,000 records per closure (`MAX_CLOSURE_RECORDS`). `readPublicRecord` builds the same snapshot from it as the publish check builds from the tables (`buildSnapshot` with a `SnapshotSource`: `tableSource` or `publicSource`), so both see records, templates and identifiers the same way; core then keeps only what is public (`effectiveRecord` → `publicView` → `publicOutput`). The rows still hold the staff values of those few records (core needs them to decide inheritance and visibility), so the reader can see staff values of a public record's own ancestry and link targets, and nothing else. *Rejected:* filtering staff values in SQL (a second implementation of core's visibility and inheritance rules to keep in step); views over each table (the existing queries name `public.*` tables, and a view still needs a gate per row); a schema or role per site (sites are rows, made by system managers at any time).
- **The public reader role.** `grantPublicReaderRole(tx, role)` gives USAGE on schema `public` and nothing else. EXECUTE on the function is PUBLIC's, as for every function (an explicit grant would be an object privilege the allowlist refuses; a revoke from PUBLIC is one the backup module won't restore, D-064). `checkPublicReaderConnection(db)` is `checkWriterConnection`'s allowlist (D-067, S-015) with **no table privilege at all, not even SELECT**, and must be able to EXECUTE `PUBLIC_READER_FUNCTIONS`; every other rule is the writer's (no attributes, memberships, ownership, column or default privileges, CREATE, parameters, extra settings, replica mode). **T-050/T-051 connect the public sites with such a role, run the check at startup and after every migration, and refuse to serve when it reports anything.** Tested: the reader is refused `42501` on records, versions, users, sessions, second factors, templates and holdings; the check passes for it and reports `extra_privilege` for a role with SELECT on every table and for the writer.
- **What any connecting role can learn through the function:** the output inputs of any record public on any site, which is what public sites show anyway (staff values of those records' ancestry and link targets included, as above). That is why only trusted roles may connect at all (D-067's rule stands).
- Sources: PostgreSQL 16 docs `sql-createfunction.html` ("Writing SECURITY DEFINER Functions Safely": pin `search_path`, `pg_temp` last), `ddl-priv.html` (PUBLIC's default EXECUTE on functions, USAGE on schema `public`), `queries-with.html`; read 2026-11-24.
- **Review fixes (2026-11-24, manual session).** (1) *No other owner's-privilege functions.* `checkPublicReaderConnection` now also refuses, as `security_definer_function`, any SECURITY DEFINER function in any schema that the role can execute (through PUBLIC or a grant) other than `PUBLIC_READER_DEFINERS`: `public.public_record_snapshot(uuid, uuid, uuid)` and 0014's `public.second_step_next_order()` (calling it only skips numbers of an ordering sequence). Such a function runs with its owner's privileges, so any other one would be a way past "no table privilege". The writer's check is unchanged. Tested with an owner-made SECURITY DEFINER function, refused until it is dropped. (2) *Link targets carry only what core reads.* Rows outside the record's own chain (the records its values link to, and their ancestry) now come back with `fields` `{}`, the title `Linked record` and no coordinates: core reads only their kind, place in the tree, template and whether they are public (link checks, deed checks, place checks). Tested: the snapshot holds the link target's ID but not its title or values, and the public output is unchanged (every public-output test passes as before). **What a blanked row still sends** (final review, L2): its ID, organization, home site, kind, level, agent type, media type, record type and template keys, its parent and represented record (so its ancestry can be walked), its creation and change times, and whether it is public on the site. **For T-018b and T-034e:** when accessions are stored, the register core builds for the public output reads linked records' ancestry only today (`linkDeedCheck`); if it ever needs a linked record's fields (or a deed check of a linked record reads them), `public_record_snapshot` must send what it reads, and a test must show it.

## D-081 — Sign-in over HTTP and the admin's first screens: JSON routes beside the staff API with the CSRF token of whichever session the cookie names, fixed codes, a per-address brake, startup refusals for the key, the client address and the reset sender, and the built admin served from memory on its own origin with its own CSP (2026-11-24, manual session, T-045)
Builds T-045 on D-034, D-052, D-058, D-068, D-069, D-072/D-074, D-073, D-077, D-078 and D-079. `packages/api/src/sign-in-api.ts` (`signInRoutes`), `admin-files.ts`, `admin-server.ts` (`buildAdminServer`), `admin-cli.ts` (`npm run admin`); `packages/db/src/sign-in-screens.ts`; the new workspace `packages/admin`. No migration. One new runtime dependency: `uqr` 0.1.3 (MIT, no dependencies) for the QR code, as D-052 and D-077 suggested; the rest are D-073's.
- **Routes** (all JSON, `application/json` only; every post `credentials: true`, so 8 KiB, and D-058's Origin check, which is the Login form's protection against login CSRF):
  - `POST /api/sign-in` `{ email, password }` → `beginSession`; the waiting session's token in the cookie; `200 { expiresAt, next: "code" | "set_up", setUpLinkRequired, csrfToken }`. A wrong password, an unknown address, a disabled account and one without an organization are the same `401 invalid_credentials` (D-068); `429 throttled` with `Retry-After`. The new session is made first; then the session the browser already held (if its cookie came with the request) is ended, so one sign-in leaves one session.
  - `GET /api/sign-in/pending` → which screen is next and the waiting session's CSRF token (`pendingSignInState`), for a reload.
  - `POST /api/sign-in/code` `{ code | recoveryCode, organizationId? }` → `completeSecondStep`; `POST /api/sign-in/set-up` `{ setUpLink? }` → `startEnrolment` (the secret and `otpauth://` link, once); `POST /api/sign-in/set-up/confirm` `{ code, organizationId? }` → `confirmEnrolment` (the full session in the cookie and the 10 recovery codes, once). A right code that still needs an organization is `409 { error: "choose_organization", organizations: [{ organizationId, name }] }` (`organizationChoices`: the person's memberships, or every organization for a system admin with none, `organization_required`); nothing was used up, so the screen sends the same code again with one.
  - `POST /api/sign-out` (either stage; without a cookie it only clears it), `POST /api/sign-out/others` (`endOtherSessions`), `POST /api/organization` `{ organizationId }` (`switchOrganization`: a new session and token, D-019), `GET /api/me` (the person's name and address, the organization's name, the session's limits, the CSRF token, and `listMySites`).
  - `POST /api/password-reset` `{ email }` → `202 { requested: true }` for every address let through (D-078's one reply; the outcome goes to the log as a fixed code, never with the address or token); `POST /api/password-reset/complete` `{ token, password }` → `200 { reset: true }` (no cookie: the person signs in afresh), every unusable link the same `400 invalid_reset`, D-068's password rules as `422 invalid_password` with core's fixed issues.
- **CSRF where a session exists.** Every post that acts on a session, waiting or full, needs `X-Oculus-CSRF` equal to `csrfTokenFor` of **that** cookie's token, checked in constant time before the database (`403 csrf_failed`). The token is only in same-origin `no-store` JSON replies (sign-in, pending, code, set-up confirm, switch, `/api/me`); the admin keeps it in memory only. Posts without a session (sign-in, reset request and completion) rely on the Origin check, as D-069 planned. *Rejected:* a CSRF cookie (D-069 keeps the token out of cookies); no CSRF on the waiting stage (sign-out and the set-up start would then be forgeable from a same-site page that passes `Sec-Fetch-Site`).
- **Screens report what really happened** (review): signing out (and cancelling a sign-in) says so only after a 2xx or a 401 (no session left); any other failure says the session is still alive and offers Try again. A code resent with the chosen organization that has run out meanwhile (`invalid_code`) brings the code field back, keeping the choice.
- **Fixed codes.** `invalid_code` 422; `too_many_attempts` 401 (the waiting session has ended); `set_up_required`, `already_set_up`, `use_recovery_code` (the secret can't be opened or its chain is used up), `same_organization` 409; `set_up_link_required`, `not_permitted`, `reauthentication_required`, `csrf_failed` 403; `unauthenticated` 401 for no session, an ended one and the wrong stage alike; `retry`, `timeout`, `unavailable`, `busy`, `client_address_unknown` 503; `rate_limited` 429. Each reply is built from the database's code alone.
- **Two brakes.** The database's own throttles decide (D-068, D-077, D-078); in front of them a cheap in-memory limit per client address (core's `throttleSourceKey`, so an IPv6 /64 counts once; `credentials`, 60 a minute) on every one of these routes, checked before any session lookup (review: sign-out, the waiting session's state, `/api/me`, switching and signing out everywhere else too), and D-079's per-session limit on the routes of a full session. Either answers `429 rate_limited` with `Retry-After: 60` (a window's length). Per process, like D-079's.
- **The client address (D-068's T-045 note).** A request whose address isn't an IP address (a Unix socket) is refused `503 client_address_unknown` and logged, never counted on a shared `source:other`. **`buildAdminServer` refuses to start in production** unless `OCULUS_TRUSTED_PROXY` names the proxy or the new `OCULUS_CLIENT_ADDRESS=direct` says the server faces clients itself; in production a request from a loopback address also logs a `client_address_warning` (at most every 10 minutes), the sign of a proxy that isn't configured. Production is anything but `NODE_ENV=development` or `test`, so a missing `NODE_ENV` gets the strict checks.
- **Other startup refusals.** `loadSecretKeyring` first (D-077; the error names the variable, never the value); no `deliverReset` in production (T-040 will supply the sender; in development the default logs only `reset_link_produced`, never the token or address); `OCULUS_ADMIN_ORIGIN` (new) not one of `OCULUS_ORIGINS`; an admin build that can't be read. A `startup_warning` is logged when `log_parameter_max_length_on_error` isn't 0 (D-068: the database's own log would then hold bound values; Oculus's logs never write `where`, `detail` or a message, through D-079's `redactedError`).
- **Serving the admin.** `loadAdminFiles` reads `packages/admin/dist` once at startup into memory (regular files only, a fixed list of types, no dot files or links, at most 500 files and 32 MiB); `buildServer({ admin: { origin, files } })` answers `GET`/`HEAD` from that table, on the admin's origin only (scheme and host), for paths no route claims: the listed file, or `index.html` for a screen path (no `.` in its last segment); never `/api…` or `/health`. Those replies get `ADMIN_SECURITY_HEADERS`: `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; manifest-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, plus nosniff, no-referrer, `no-store` and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`. Every other reply keeps `SECURITY_HEADERS` unchanged. *Rejected:* `@fastify/static` (a plugin, D-051, reading the file system per request); a route per file (handlers may not send HTML, D-058).
- **No inline style under that CSP.** React sets styles through the CSSOM, which `style-src 'self'` allows. React Aria's press handling inserts one `<style>` element; the admin serves the same rule from `/react-aria.css` and links it with the id React Aria looks for, so it inserts nothing (the end-to-end test fails on any CSP report). React Aria's iOS modal scroll lock inserts another (`overscroll-behavior: contain`), which the CSP refuses harmlessly; no modal is used yet. *Rejected:* a hash or `'unsafe-inline'` in `style-src`.
- **The screens** (`packages/admin`): Login (mockup Login; an honest placeholder in place of its photograph), the code (or a recovery code), choosing an organization (radio cards), setting up (QR drawn as SVG from React elements with `uqr`, the key in groups of four, then the recovery codes once with "I have saved these codes" before going on), "Forgot your password?" (one message for every address), choosing a new password from `/reset#<token>` (the fragment is read once and removed from the address bar), and the frame (DESIGN.md §5: the sidebar with the Oculus wordmark, the organization's name and the site selector grouped by organization; the top bar with the person's initials and name and a menu to sign out or sign out everywhere else; a skip link). After sign-in, exactly one site opens directly; several give a "Choose a site" page; none says whom to ask. A site of another organization switches organization first. Secrets and recovery codes live only in screen state, never in storage or TanStack Query's cache. Paragraph text is sized in `rem` (the tokens' pixel sizes at the default text size) so it follows the reader's setting.
- **Tests.** HTTP tests for every route (no session, a waiting session where a full one is needed and the reverse, missing and wrong CSRF, a foreign or missing Origin, the per-address limit and the database's throttles, the happy paths, identical replies for a wrong password and an unknown address and for reset requests, a reset after a system admin's reset of two-step sign-in needing the emailed code); the startup refusals and the admin files; screen tests in Vitest with jsdom and Testing Library; and **a new gate step after the build**: Playwright drives the built admin served by Fastify with its own throwaway database (`e2e/serve.mjs`: sign in, set up with a TOTP code computed by core, recovery codes, the only site, sign out by keyboard) and runs Siteimprove Alfa's rules on each screen. Rules whose success criteria are all at level AAA (1.4.6 enhanced contrast and the like) are left out, since Oculus aims at AA; techniques and best practices stay in. It took about 7 seconds on this 2-core machine, 7 runs in 7 passing. CI installs Playwright's Chromium; elsewhere `OCULUS_E2E_BROWSER` or a system Chrome at a usual path is used. Keyboard and screen-reader checks are manual: `packages/admin/README.md` has the checklist.
- **The license check** now also finds every font file in the repository (outside dependencies and builds): each must be listed in a `FONTS.json` beside it with its SHA-256, an allowed license (OFL-1.1 for fonts only, or anything D-002 allows) and its license text in the same folder. OFL-1.1 stays refused for npm packages. The five files are fontsource's unmodified Latin subsets (Roboto 500/600/700, Open Sans 400/600, from `@fontsource/roboto` and `@fontsource/open-sans` 5.3.0, fetched with `npm pack`, never installed).
- **Left for later:** T-040's email must carry the set-up code after an admin's reset (the screen asks the person to type it; a link such as `/sign-in/set-up#<token>` could fill it) and the reset link as `/reset#<token>`; T-050 limits the API routes to the admin's host (today only the admin files check the host; no public site is served yet); T-048 shows a person's own sessions and the "someone is guessing your codes" notice; the top bar's search and "+ Catalog an item" come with their screens.
- Sources: OWASP ASVS 5.0 V3.3 (cookies), V3.4 (security headers), V3.5 (CSRF), V6.3.8 and V7.2.4, as already cited in D-052, D-058 and D-069; MDN Content-Security-Policy (`style-src` and the CSSOM, `form-action`, `frame-ancestors`), Fetch metadata and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`; WCAG 2.1 (1.4.4 Resize text, 2.4.1 Bypass blocks, 2.4.7 Focus visible); the React Aria Components 1.22 and `uqr` 0.1.3 sources in `node_modules`; Siteimprove Alfa 0.120 (`@siteimprove/alfa-rules`, `alfa-act`, `alfa-playwright`), read 2026-11-24.

## D-082 — The staff API's history, restore, workflow and approval-queue routes: history needs `records.history` where the record is now, versions answered missing alike, the moves as their own POST routes with `If-Match`, the queue over whole publish sites (2026-11-24, T-043)
Builds on D-079 (the staff API's session, CSRF, origin, size, type, rate-limit, `X-Oculus-Site` and reply rules, all unchanged), D-065 (compare and restore), D-072/D-074 (permissions, sites), D-075/D-076 (workflow) and S-020/S-028 (the hidden-source checks and the lock order). `packages/api/src/staff-api.ts`; `listVersions`, `readRecordVersion`, `compareRecordVersions` and `reviewQueue` in `packages/service/src/service.ts`; `recordsInState` in `packages/db/src/workflow.ts`. Migration `0019_review_queue_index.sql` (a partial index for the queue, review item 1); no new dependency. The service stays the only writer (D-067); the routes call only the service.
- **Endpoints (admin hostname only):**
  - `GET /api/records/:id/versions?limit=&after=`: the history, oldest first, **without content**: each version's number, actor, time, changed field names, reason, action (`save`, `delete`, or one of the four workflow actions) and the workflow state after it. `limit` 1 to 100 (default 50), `next` is the last version listed when there are more, else null.
  - `GET /api/records/:id/versions/:version`: one version with its whole record (every field, staff fields included).
  - `GET /api/records/:id/compare?from=&to=`: `compareVersions` (D-065), the differences with both values.
  - `POST /api/records/:id/versions/:version/restore`: `If-Match: "v<current>"`, optional `{ reason, confirmMadePublic }`; the service's `restoreVersion` unchanged (history and edit permissions, its move checks, the S-020 hidden-source refusal for published records, S-028's below/record/above lock order, `confirmMadePublic` asking `records.publish` on a published record, D-076). 200 `{ record, version }` with the new ETag.
  - `POST /api/records/:id/submit-for-review`, `/return-to-draft`, `/publish`, `/withdraw`: `If-Match: "v<current>"`, optional `{ reason }`; 200 `{ state, version, actor, recordedAt }` with the new ETag. The service's checks unchanged: the first two need the edit permission (`records.edit`, or `media.attach` for a scan), `publish` and `withdraw` need `records.publish` through `mayChangePublicOutput`; `publish` runs the publish check and S-020's hidden-source check in its transaction. Moves D-075 doesn't allow are 422 with core's issue. *Rejected:* one `POST /api/records/:id/workflow { action }` (one route whose permission depends on a body field; separate routes say what they do in the access log and in 405s).
  - `GET /api/review-queue?limit=&after=`: the approval queue, records `ready_for_review` (ID, kind, title, home site, version, change time), in ID order, `next` the last ID when there are more.
  - `GET /api/records/:id` now also returns the record's `state` (read under the same row lock), so the admin knows which moves to offer.
- **Who sees history.** `records.view` and `records.history` at the record's place **now** (home site, units; D-072), checked under its row lock `FOR SHARE` like `readRecord`. Read-only people and Scanners see the record but no old version (`403 not_permitted`; John, 2026-11-24, D-072); people who can't see the record get the same `404 not_found` as for a missing record. A deleted record has no row to check a place at, so its history is `404` here (a system manager's audit view of deleted records is T-048's). *Accepted:* old versions are the record's, so they are shown whatever site or unit the record was in when they were written: a record a system manager moved from site A2 to A1 shows its A2-era versions to A1's catalogers, and an old version may name a parent, linked record or site the reader can't see now (IDs only, as a record's current links already may, D-072). *Rejected:* checking each version's own place (a record's history would have holes depending on who looks, and an old place may no longer exist).
- **Missing versions say nothing before the permission.** A version number that is malformed, out of range or not stored (`/versions/x`, `/versions/0`, `/versions/99999`, `compare?from=1` without `to`) is passed to the service as a version the record doesn't have, which answers `unknown_version` only after the history check. The route maps `unknown_version`/`invalid_version` on `version`, `from` or `to` to the same `404 not_found` as a missing record, so `/records/<hidden>/versions/99` and `/records/<missing>/versions/1` are byte for byte the same, and a read-only person gets 403 for every version number of a record they see.
- **The approval queue** is for people who may publish: a system manager every record of the organization; anyone else the whole sites where they hold `records.publish` for the whole catalog (every site for a grant on every site; never records on no site), filtered in the query (D-079's reason: a page filtered afterwards would show by its length that hidden records matched). Someone with no `records.publish` anywhere is `403`. Scoped publish grants (a unit's subtree) don't add records yet, as with search; a scoped reviewer gets an empty queue and opens records by other routes. The site rule is now one helper (`wholeSites`) shared with search. *Rejected:* listing for catalogers too (they have their own items list, T-046); ordering by the time of submission (needs the latest version's time per record; ID order is stable for paging, and the screen can sort a page).
- **Edits of published records go live at once (John, D-076)**: `PUT /api/records/:id` on a published record needs only the edit permission, re-runs the publish check and keeps the record published; tested over HTTP with the public output read afterwards.
- **Races.** A publish and an edit (or any two changes) from the same version serialize on the record's row lock; the second gets `412 version_conflict` with the names-only conflict and writes nothing. Pinned over HTTP in both orders by holding the row `FOR UPDATE` on a third connection until both requests wait.
- **Review fixes (2026-11-24).**
  - *B1, restore's first look.* `restoreVersion` read the record's place and then its old version before any permission check, so a restore of a hidden record took longer than one of a missing record, and longer the bigger the old version. Now the first look asks `allowsUnlocked` (`records.history` and the edit permission; system managers always pass) before reading the version: a record the person can't view is `unknown_record` at once, with no version read and no lock taken (this also drops the arrangement and row locks a hidden unit's refusal used to take); one they see but may not restore reads no version and is refused by the full check under the row lock, which is unchanged. If that full check allows what the first look refused (a grant in between), the restore starts again (`retry`), since nothing was decided from the old version. Tested by recording every statement: no hidden or refused restore touches `record_versions`, and the allowed control does. No timing test.
  - *B2, the cost of the list.* `listVersions` read and re-validated every full snapshot (100 versions of about 900 KB took 570–750 ms and read about 90 MB) to send summaries. It now reads only the small columns (`readVersionSummaries`: number, actor, time, changed names, reason, action, state; checked for shape), never `record`. Tested: no statement of the list names the column, and the list still answers when a stored snapshot no longer validates (reading that version is still a 500).
  - *The queue* lists through `records_ready_for_review`, a partial index on `(organization_id, id) WHERE workflow_state = 'ready_for_review'` like 0012's `records_published`, under the same `statement_timeout` as search (`searchTimeoutMs`); a list stopped by it is `503 timeout` (tested by holding a lock on `records`).
  - *What history shows* (accepted): each version's `actor` is another staff member's internal user ID (`user:<uuid>`, opaque, of the same organization), and old versions carry old site IDs and parent or link IDs (opaque, same organization). The admin turns IDs into names only where the person may see them (T-048).
  - *Locks first* (accepted, as T-042's `GET /api/records/:id`): history reads and the restore's checks lock the record's row `FOR SHARE` or `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` before checking permissions, so a lock someone else holds on a hidden record makes these requests wait (and only then answer 404), which a missing record doesn't. The unlocked first look above removes this for restores of hidden records; the history reads keep it, like `readRecord`.
- **Logs** are D-079's: only the error's class and SQLSTATE or system code, through `serviceErrorLogger`.
- **Tests** (`packages/api/src/staff-api.history.test.ts`, HTTP over a fresh migrated database): each new endpoint with no session, a waiting, expired or made-up session (401), CSRF missing, wrong or another session's (403), a foreign or no Origin (403), 413, three wrong content types (415), unknown body keys (422), `If-Match` missing (428) or malformed (400); a table of every built-in role (and a system manager, another site's cataloger and another organization's member) for history, restore, submit, publish and withdraw, and a Scanner on a scan; identical replies for a missing, another site's, another organization's, a deleted and a malformed record on every endpoint, and for unknown or malformed versions; restore with ETag, the conflict, `no_changes`, an unknown version; the moves in order with the history they leave; the publish check refusing; an edit of a published record going live; S-020's refusal over HTTP for a scoped reviewer publishing under a hidden fonds and restoring a published file to a version under one (no ID or value of the hidden record in the reply, nothing changed); the queue per role and site with paging; the two pinned races; and that the log holds no record text or ID.
- **Left for later:** the organization-wide change log and audit trail (T-048: who changed what across records, deleted records' histories, role changes); scoped grants in the queue and search; ordering the queue by submission time; T-035's optional approval step (when on, `publish` only from `ready_for_review`), which changes the service, not these routes.
- Sources: RFC 9110 §13.1.1 (`If-Match`), §15.5.5 (404 for a resource the server won't disclose), §15.5.13; OWASP ASVS 5.0 V8 (deny by default, check at the trusted service layer, answer what may not be seen as if it didn't exist); as already cited in D-079.

## D-083 — The admin's item screens: a staff items list over whole sites with every filter in SQL and offset pages, a record's abilities and inherited values in its read, the editor's checks and conflicts from the server, and confirmations in the page instead of modals (2026-11-24, T-046)
Builds T-046 on D-073 (the admin stack), D-079 and D-082 (the staff API), D-072/D-074 (permissions and sites), D-075/D-076 (workflow) and D-081 (the admin's CSP and e2e step). `packages/db/src/record-list.ts` (new), `search.ts`, `workflow.ts` (`inheritedValues`); `listItems`, `listRecordTypes`, `siteAbilities` and `readRecord` in `packages/service/src/service.ts`; three routes in `packages/api/src/staff-api.ts`; `packages/admin/src/records/`. No migration, no new dependency.
- **`GET /api/records?q=&state=&type=&sort=&after=&limit=`** (the search bucket, 60 a minute): the working site's records (`X-Oculus-Site`; a site the person has no role on is `unknown_site`, as a made-up one), only of whole sites they may see every record of (D-079's rule, the same `wholeSites`), so scoped grants still add nothing. With `q` it is staff search (D-066) narrowed by `state` and `type` in the same SQL, best match first, with search's cursor; without, `listRecords`: last changed first or by title, **offset pages** (`after` is a number, at most 10,000), so no title or time ever travels back in a request or a log line. Every filter is in the query, never applied to a page afterwards, so a page's length says nothing about other records. Under the search `statement_timeout`. *Rejected:* keyset paging by title (the cursor would carry record text); filtering search results in the service (short pages would show that hidden records matched); a separate list endpoint per filter.
- **`GET /api/records/:id` adds `can` and `inherited`.** `can: { edit, history, publish, delete }` is the operations' own checks (`RecordAccess.allows` at the record's place, under the same `FOR SHARE` row lock as the read), so the admin offers only what the person may do; every operation still checks for itself. `inherited` lists the values the record takes now from the units above it (D-013's effective record, staff values included: the reader is staff), each with the record it comes from; it is **empty when any unit above is out of the person's sight** (a system manager filed it across sites), because even "a value comes through" would say something about it. **It fails closed** (review): every record above is locked `FOR SHARE` (after the record's own row lock, as S-020's `lockAbove`) and the set read again before the values are read, so nothing above can move, change or be deleted between reading them and checking who may see their sources; the list is also empty when the set changed meanwhile or when the effective record names a record above that isn't among those locked (a check of visibility skips records that no longer exist, so a unit deleted mid-read must not count as visible). Tested with a hook between the read and the visibility check that moves the series out from under a hidden fonds and deletes the fonds. *Rejected:* a separate effective-record endpoint now (the editor needs only these values; the Effective Record screen can add one).
- **`GET /api/working-site`**: `{ createKinds, review }`: the kinds the person may make there (all six with `records.create` on the site, scoped grants included; only `digital_asset` with `media.attach` alone; none otherwise) and whether they may publish anywhere (the approval queue's own rule). **`GET /api/record-types`**: the organization's record types, for anyone who may read templates.
- **The editor.** One input per field kind (D-073): text, long text and dates as text fields, pick lists as selects, structured kinds as labelled groups of parts, repeatable fields as lists. Checks come only from `POST /api/records/check` (on leaving a field, and before a save; a field's problems show once it has been touched, all of them after a save is tried). Saves send `If-Match`; a 412 names the changed fields (names only, D-082) and offers "Reload the latest version", which discards the local edits and says so; nothing is retried or forced. A published record's editor says before saving that the change goes live at once (John, D-076). Values the record holds for keys its template no longer has are sent back unchanged. Workflow moves are offered from D-075's table and `can` (the edit moves need `edit`, publishing needs `publish`), and wait while there are unsaved changes; Delete needs `can.delete` (site managers, John). The history panel shows only with `can.history` (never read-only people or Scanners); restoring asks first, and when the server answers `public_field_restored` it asks again naming those fields and resends with exactly them as `confirmMadePublic`. Leaving with unsaved changes asks (React Router's `useBlocker`, and `beforeunload`).
- **Confirmations are in the page, not modal dialogs.** A modal's overlay and focus trap hide the page's skip link and its only `h1` (Alfa's SIA-R61 and R87 fail) and dim the text behind it below AA contrast as Alfa measures it (SIA-R69). The confirmation is a labelled region placed beside its control; focus moves to it, Tab reaches its buttons, Escape cancels. This also keeps React Aria's modal scroll lock (which injects a `<style>` on WebKit, D-081) out of the admin.
- **The page's address holds only the site and the record ID.** Search words and filters live in the tab's memory (a module map), never in the address, storage or `history.state`. **One helper, `endSession` (`src/session.ts`), forgets a session's data**: the CSRF token, the whole TanStack Query cache and the lists' search words and filters. It runs on signing out, when `GET /api/me` answers 401, when any query or mutation meets `401 unauthenticated` on a screen that needs a session (a global `QueryCache`/`MutationCache` `onError`, which also goes to sign in), and when a new session begins after the second step or set-up (keeping the new session's token), so the next person in the same tab sees nothing of the last one's (review). Switching organization drops every cached reply but the person's own (refetched), and the templates' and record types' query keys name the organization. The record's query never refetches on reconnect or focus, so the editor (which starts a fresh form for each version) can't lose typed edits to a background refetch; notices passed between screens carry no record text. Record text is rendered as React text only; no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; the only links built from data are a record's route (from an ID matching the UUID form) and, where needed, `safeHref` (http and https only, no credentials).
- **Tests.** HTTP tests for the list (site, filters, sort, paging, search, other sites and organizations, malformed parameters), the abilities of every role and the inherited values with a hidden fonds; screen tests for the list, the queue and the editor; and the e2e step now also signs in an editor and goes through search, edit, a 412 made by a concurrent save, save, publish, history and restore by keyboard with Alfa on each state.
- **Left for later:** editing identifier numbers (the ID numbers tab: `saveIdentifiers`), moving a record to another template (`confirmTemplateMove`) and confirming dropped numbers (`confirmDropped`): the editor keeps the template and numbers as they are; pickers for controlled terms and record links (React Aria ComboBox over a vocabulary search and the list); scoped grants in the list and the queue (D-079); naming who made a version (the history says "You" or "Someone else" until a people lookup exists, T-048).
- Sources: Siteimprove Alfa 0.120's results in this run (SIA-R61, R69, R87 on a React Aria modal); React Router 8's `useBlocker` (its declarations in `node_modules`); React Aria 1.22's `usePreventScroll` source; otherwise as already cited in D-073, D-079, D-081 and D-082 (RFC 9110 `If-Match`, OWASP ASVS 5.0 V8).

## D-084 — People, roles and the audit trail: system managers act on accounts, site managers on their own sites' roles, every change to people in an append-only log, a fresh password-and-code entry for changes, and an audit trail filtered in the query by what the viewer may see (2026-11-24, T-048)
Builds T-048 on D-019, D-025, D-067 to D-069, D-072, D-074, D-077 to D-079, D-081 and D-082. `packages/db/src/people.ts`, `packages/db/migrations/0020_people_events.sql`; `packages/service/src/people.ts` (and `grantRoleIn`/`revokeRoleIn` taken out of `admin.ts` unchanged, so the people service can log in the same transaction); `packages/api/src/people-api.ts` (mounted by `staffApiRoutes` when given `people`), `POST /api/reauthenticate` in `sign-in-api.ts`; the admin's `src/people/` screens. No new dependency. (D-083 is T-046's.)
- **Endpoints (admin hostname only, D-079's session, CSRF, origin, size, type and per-session rules unchanged):** `GET /api/people`, `GET /api/people/:id`, `GET /api/roles`, `POST /api/invitations` `{ email, expiresInDays? }`, `POST /api/people/:id/disable` and `/enable`, `POST /api/people/:id/roles` `{ role, siteId, scope }`, `DELETE /api/role-assignments/:id`, `POST /api/people/:id/reset-two-step` `{ code | recoveryCode }`, `POST /api/people/:id/password-reset` `{ confirmMailboxOnly? }`, `POST /api/people/:id/end-sessions`, `GET /api/audit?person=&record=&from=&to=&after=&limit=`, and `POST /api/reauthenticate` `{ password, code | recoveryCode }` (a credentials route: 8 KiB, per-address brake; a wrong password is `403 invalid_credentials`, not 401, since the session lives on; success writes the new token and returns its CSRF token). People have no versions, so no `If-Match`.
- **Who may do what.** *Seeing people:* system managers see every member of the session's organization, with all their roles, their live sessions (ID, organization, stage, times and label: never a token, hash or address) and two-step status, including the D-077 wrong-code alerts of the last 30 days. Site managers (`site.people`) see the members holding a role on a site they manage or holding no role at all, and only the roles they may manage (none on every site, no site manager grants: D-072's listing rule). Anyone else is `403`. *Roles:* exactly `grantRole`/`revokeRole` (D-072, backed by 0018's trigger). *Accounts* (inviting, disabling and re-enabling, resetting two-step sign-in, sending a reset link, ending every session): system managers only, since accounts are install-wide (D-019, D-068); site managers can't invite (0009's trigger still requires a system admin as inviter; widening it is later work). Every account action is limited to **members of the session's organization**: the service checks membership first, and passes the organization to `endUserSessions`, `resetSecondFactor` and `sendPasswordReset` as their new `memberOf` option, which checks it again inside the transaction that acts (for the two-step reset, after the user rows are locked), so the check and the action can't drift apart; a non-member is `unknown_user` there, `404` over HTTP.
- **Answers that say nothing.** A member out of view, another organization's member, an unknown ID and a malformed one are the same `404 not_found`, byte for byte (tested); an assignment the person can't manage and a missing one too. The order of answers is: permission first (`not_permitted`), then the person (`unknown_person`), then the fresh entry (`reauthentication_required`). A malformed person ID is the same `404` on every route, the grant included. Invitations never say whether the address has an account elsewhere (D-068; tested by comparing the replies for an address with an account in another organization and a new one); `already_member` is answered only to system managers, who see every member anyway.
- **A fresh entry for changes (D-072's left item).** Granting, revoking, disabling, re-enabling and ending sessions need the password and a code entered in the last 10 minutes. For granting, revoking, disabling and re-enabling the service reads the acting session from its token in the same transaction (`actingSession`, which uses D-069's `readActingSession`) and requires it to be the actor's own; for ending sessions, resetting two-step sign-in and sending a reset link the 10-minute check is the database function's own (`endUserSessions`, `resetSecondFactor`, `sendPasswordReset`). A grant or revocation is checked for permission first and for the fresh entry after, in the same transaction, so a refused one writes nothing. Inviting doesn't ask (it changes no one's access until accepted). The admin shows the re-entry form in the page and retries the change.
- **The last system manager.** 0020's `users_last_system_admin` (BEFORE UPDATE OF `disabled_at`, ALWAYS) refuses disabling an active system admin while no other active one exists, locking the others' rows first; `setAccountDisabled` answers it `last_system_manager`. Today 0009 allows only the bootstrap account to be a system admin, so it can never be disabled; making and removing system managers is still left for later (D-068, D-072), and D-077's note (the writer must never be able to make system admins) still binds it.
- **D-078's warning.** `password-reset` for someone with no factor in use (never set up, or reset and not set up again) is `409 confirmation_required` with `confirm_mailbox_only` unless `confirmMailboxOnly: true`; the screen says that whoever reads the mailbox could take over the account and asks the manager to confirm the person's identity another way (a checkbox) before the button works.
- **The change log (0020).** `people_events (organization_id, id, occurred_at, actor_id, subject_id, kind, role, site_id, scope_record_id, invitation_id)`: kinds `invited`, `account_disabled`, `account_enabled`, `role_granted`, `role_revoked`, `second_factor_reset`, `password_reset_sent`, `sessions_ended`; a CHECK fixes which columns each kind carries; stamped now (trigger), append-only for every writer (UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE refused by ALWAYS triggers), the writer gets INSERT only (`PEOPLE_TABLE_PRIVILEGES` in `WRITER_PRIVILEGES`). Role changes and disabling write their row **in the same transaction** as the change; invitations, two-step resets, reset links and ending sessions write it **right after** the database function's own transaction (those functions commit before they send mail, D-068, D-077, D-078), so a crash between the two loses the line; a failed write goes to `onError`. Outcomes after which a message may have gone or a link or invitation may still be live are logged too (review): an invitation answered `superseded`, `revoked`, `delivery_failed` or `delivery_failed_may_be_pending` (its ID found as the inviter's newest invitation for that address since the call began), and a reset link answered `delivery_failed_may_be_pending` or `superseded`. *Accepted:* the writer can add false lines (never change or remove true ones). *Rejected:* changing the reviewed account functions to take a logging hook (more surface in D-068 to D-078's code for one line).
- **The audit trail (D-082 left it here).** One query, newest first, over `record_versions` (every save, deletion and workflow move; deleted records' versions stay, D-067) and `people_events`, keyset-paged by time and a key (`<time to the microsecond>_<r<record>:<version> | e<event>>`), filtered by actor, record and a date range, **with the viewer's scope in the SQL** so a page's length never shows that hidden rows matched (D-079's reason). Records: a system manager every version; anyone else the versions of records whose **current** home site is one where they hold `records.history` for the whole catalog (D-082: a record's history goes where the record is now; scoped grants don't add, as in search and the queue). **A deleted record has no home site to check, so its history is system managers' only.** People events: all of the organization's for a system manager; for a site manager the role grants and revocations on the sites they manage, never site manager grants, every-site grants or account events. Someone with neither is `403`. A filter on a record they can't see answers exactly as one on a missing record (an empty page). Names are given only for actors and subjects who are members of the organization or system admins; anyone else is an opaque ID ("Someone outside this organization" on screen). Each line carries the record's title as of that version and the changed field names, never field values. Two indexes: `people_events_time` and `record_versions_time`. The trail runs under search's `statement_timeout` (`searchTimeoutMs`; `503 timeout` when it fires) and in the `search` rate bucket. A cursor or date that names no real moment (month 13, 30 February, year 0) is `422`, never a database error.
- **The old role operations.** `grantRole` and `revokeRole` stay on the service for set-up code and tests, marked internal; routes and screens use `grantPersonRole` and `revokePersonRole` (fresh entry and change log). The people list reads every member's roles in one query.
- **Roles screen.** Custom roles (creating and editing them, system managers only) are T-041a's and not built here: there is no storage for them and every check (core, the service, 0018's trigger) knows built-in roles only. The Roles screen lists the built-in roles and their permissions read-only and says roles of one's own come later. *Rejected:* building custom roles inside T-048 (T-041a's own risky scope: storage, resolution in every check, the trigger, deletion with people moved).
- **Screens** (`packages/admin/src/people/`): People, a person's page (roles with grant and remove, two-step sign-in, password reset link, sessions, account), Invite, Roles and Audit trail; sidebar links appear only when the server answers the matching read. Every action appears only where the person's rights allow it, and the server decides again. Dangerous actions open an **in-page confirmation** (a focused `section` with its own heading and buttons), never a modal (T-046 found React Aria's modals failing Alfa). Names and other people's text are rendered as text only. Native `select` and date inputs with explicit `label for`. No token, link or code is ever shown (the set-up and reset messages go only to the person's mailbox).
- **Delivery.** `buildAdminServer` takes `deliverInvitation` and `deliverEnrolment` (T-040 makes the emails); in development each logs only that a message was made (`invitation_produced`, `set_up_link_produced`), never its token or address; in production, without one, the matching route answers `503 delivery_unavailable` (startup is not refused, so T-045's startup contract is unchanged).
- **Tests.** HTTP over a fresh database (`people-api.test.ts`): every route without a session, with a waiting session, without or with another session's CSRF token, from another origin; who sees whom; byte-identical 404s; escalation attempts by a site manager (site manager grants for others and themselves, every-site grants, other sites, another organization's site) and by people without `site.people`, all refused with nothing written; 0018 still refusing a forged grant; the fresh-entry rule and `POST /api/reauthenticate`; account actions refused to site managers on anyone, system managers included; the last system manager; disabling ending sessions and re-enabling reviving none; two-step reset with the admin's own code and the set-up token in no reply or log; D-078's warning; invitation tokens in no reply or log; no enumeration by address; the audit trail per viewer (system manager, site manager, cataloger, read-only, another organization), deleted records, hidden-record filters, person and date filters and paging; and a log holding no token, address or title. Screen tests (`People.test.tsx`), and an end-to-end test (`e2e/people.spec.ts`) by keyboard: sign in as the system manager, invite, give a role, reset two-step sign-in with a recovery code, see the D-078 warning, read the audit trail and the roles, with Alfa on each screen.
- Sources: OWASP ASVS 5.0 V8 (deny by default, check at the trusted service layer, answer what may not be seen as if it didn't exist), V7.5.1 (re-authentication before sensitive changes), V16 (security logging: who did what and when, no secrets in logs); OWASP Logging Cheat Sheet; PostgreSQL 16 `sql-createtrigger.html` (statement-level `BEFORE TRUNCATE`, `ENABLE ALWAYS`), `queries-limit.html` and row-value comparisons for keyset paging; as already cited in D-068, D-069, D-072, D-077 to D-079 and D-082.

## D-085 — Email: plain SMTP through nodemailer, an in-memory outbox off the request path with retries, a per-recipient limit and logs by error class only, messages checked for CR/LF and built from the admin origin, a development sender refused in production, two-step alert notices at most once per person per window (0021), a notice after a reset (2026-11-24, T-040)
Builds D-068's secrecy rule, D-077's enrolment delivery, D-078's reset delivery and D-081's startup refusals. `packages/api/src/mail-messages.ts` (the three messages), `mail-outbox.ts` (the outbox), `mail.ts` (the SMTP and development senders, configuration, `createMailer`, `mailerFromEnv`); `admin-server.ts` and `admin-cli.ts` use `mailerFromEnv`. Migration 0021 for the alert notices (below). **One new runtime dependency: `nodemailer` 10.6.1** (MIT-0, no dependencies of its own, its own type declarations), pinned exactly in `@oculus/api`.
- **Why nodemailer.** The most used Node SMTP client, maintained (releases this month), permissive (MIT-0 is on D-002's list), with no dependencies, and since 10.5 an `AbortSignal` per send that closes the connection (D-078 asks a send to stop when its limit fires). It also does the parts that are easy to get subtly wrong: MIME multipart, header encoding (RFC 2047), line length, dot-stuffing, SMTPUTF8, STARTTLS, AUTH. *Rejected:* our own SMTP client on `node:net`/`node:tls` (about 400 lines of protocol and MIME code to review and keep up, for no gain in control we need); `sendmail` (needs a local MTA, not on shared hosting, D-046); an HTTP mail API (ties Oculus to one provider, D-035 asks for any host's mail).
- **Configuration only from the environment**, each refusal naming the variable and never its value: `OCULUS_SMTP_HOST` (host name or IP), `OCULUS_SMTP_PORT` (587, or 465 for implicit TLS), `OCULUS_SMTP_TLS` = `starttls` (**the default: STARTTLS required**, the send fails before `MAIL FROM` if the server doesn't offer it) | `implicit` | `none` (only for a server on this machine: localhost, 127.0.0.1, ::1); TLS 1.2 or newer and the certificate always checked against the host name (`rejectUnauthorized: true` and `minVersion` are set last, after the only test lever, a CA to trust, so nothing can turn them off; review L6); `OCULUS_SMTP_USER` with `OCULUS_SMTP_PASSWORD` or `OCULUS_SMTP_PASSWORD_FILE` (a secret file; one, not both; control characters refused); `OCULUS_MAIL_FROM` (core's address rule) and `OCULUS_MAIL_FROM_NAME` (default "Oculus", no line breaks or controls). The secret keyring (`OCULUS_SECRET_KEY`, D-077) holds one sealing key, not named secrets, so the mail password comes from the environment or a file; *rejected:* sealing it with the keyring (the key and the sealed password would sit in the same environment, protecting nothing). Nodemailer's own logger and debug output are off (they write addresses and, with debug, the message), and its file and URL access are off (`disableFileAccess`, `disableUrlAccess`).
- **The messages** (`renderPasswordReset`, `renderTwoStepSetup`, `renderInvitation`): plain text plus simple HTML made from one list of paragraphs, so the two can't differ; every value in the HTML escaped (`& < > " '`), the link included; no images, style sheets, scripts or anything loaded from elsewhere (no tracking pixels); one fixed subject per kind with no token and nothing about the account. Before anything is built: the address must be exactly core's stored form (`normalizeEmail`, so no spaces, commas, angle brackets, quotes, controls), the token 43 characters of base64url (32 bytes, the one spelling), the expiry core's ISO form naming a real moment, and **no header value may hold CR, LF or any control character or line separator** (`checkHeaderValue`); an organization's name may not hold controls or direction overrides. A failed check throws `MailContentError` with a code (`bad_address`, `bad_token`, `bad_expiry`, `bad_value`, `bad_origin`), never the value. The recipient goes to nodemailer as an address object, so its address parser never reinterprets it.
  - **Links come only from `OCULUS_ADMIN_ORIGIN`** (a bare origin, https; `http://localhost` and loopback only outside production), never from a request's `Host`: `/reset#<token>` (D-081), `/invitation#<token>` (the accepting screen is T-048's), and for the set-up code only `/sign-in`: the code is shown as text to type into the set-up screen, which already asks for it (D-081), so it is never in a URL. *Rejected for now:* `/sign-in/set-up#<token>` to fill the field (needs a screen change; the code alone is enough). The fragment keeps tokens out of request lines and `Referer`.
  - Wording: a reset says who asked (the person, or a system administrator), when the link stops working (in UTC, said so), that it signs the person out everywhere and leaves two-step sign-in as it is, and to ignore it otherwise. Nothing else about the account. An invitation names the organization the inviter chose.
- **The outbox: every message is sent off the request path.** `deliverReset`, `deliverEnrolment` and `deliverInvitation` build and check the message and queue it, and return; they never wait for a mail server. With D-078's reply already sent before anything about the account is read, the mail server's speed can't show in any reply: an account and an unknown address get the same reply at the same speed even while the server stalls (tested over HTTP with the greeting delayed 1.5 s), and an unknown address queues nothing. **In memory only:** a queued message holds a bearer token, which D-068 keeps out of every table and log, so there is no outbox table. A restart drops what is queued; the person asks again. *Rejected:* a database outbox (tokens at rest in plain text, or sealed with the key that sits beside the writer role, which D-077 says must never be stored together); awaiting the send in the delivery (D-078's background work already hides it for self-service requests, but an admin's reset and an invitation would hold a request for the whole SMTP exchange, and retries couldn't wait minutes).
  - **What "delivered" now means.** A delivery resolves when the message is queued, so D-078's `sent` / `ok`, D-077's `delivered: true` and D-068's `ok` say "accepted for sending". They reject (and the link is revoked, the invitation withdrawn, `delivered: false`) only when the message can't be built or isn't queued: `MailRefusedError` `closed`, `queue_full` (500 messages queued, waiting or in flight in the message's lane) or `recipient_limit`, or an already-aborted signal. A message that later fails for good leaves its link pending until it expires (30 minutes for a reset); nobody has the token, so that is harmless, and the log says `mail_failed`.
  - **At most 2 sends at once**, each limited to 30 s by an `AbortSignal` that nodemailer honours (it closes the connection; tested with a server that never greets), plus nodemailer's own connection, greeting, socket and DNS limits (10 to 20 s). `close()` refuses new messages, drops waiting ones, aborts sends in progress and waits for them at most 10 s (review L3; tested with a send that ignores the signal); `npm run admin` closes it on SIGINT and SIGTERM.
  - **Retries with backoff and a cap.** Failures a server may get over (network, time-out, a 4xx or 503 reply, anything unrecognised) are retried after 15 s, doubling to at most 5 minutes, each wait a random half to all of that, **at most 5 sends in all** (about 4 minutes), and nothing is sent, the first try included (review L4), within a minute of the message's `notAfter`. A permanent refusal (5xx but 503), failed authentication, a TLS failure (no STARTTLS, and certificate or handshake failures, which nodemailer reports as `ESOCKET` with Node's TLS code in `originalCode` or the `cause`; review L2, tested with an untrusted certificate and a plain-text server) and bad configuration are not retried. A message superseded while it waits for a retry is still sent (its link is dead); accepted.
  - **Two lanes (review M1):** *links* (reset links, set-up codes, invitations) and *notices* (the two-step alert, the password-changed notice), each with its own capacity (500) and its own per-recipient hourly limit (links 10, notices 20), so no number of notices can keep a person's link, or an admin's own, from being queued (tested: a full notice lane and a recipient at its notice limit, then two reset links queued; and the 12-person case below). **Notices are retried far longer** (review M2): 30 s doubling to 1 hour, at most 13 sends (about 3 to 6 hours with the jitter), still bounded by the notice's own life (a day after its alert), so a short mail outage doesn't lose them.
- **A per-recipient limit:** per lane, in memory per process. This is a second line behind D-078's database counters; it bounds what a flood of requests in someone's name (or a bug) can put in their mailbox. An attacker who uses up a person's 10 can stop that person's own link from going for up to an hour; D-078's per-address counter already lets them make the person wait, and a system admin's `clearSignInThrottle` doesn't clear this one (a restart does). Accepted.
- **Logs: the kind and an error class, never an address, token, or server reply.** Fixed records `mail_sent`, `mail_retry`, `mail_failed`, `mail_refused`, `mail_dropped`, `mail_dev`, with `operation` the message kind and `code` one of `network`, `timeout`, `tls`, `auth`, `rejected_temporary`, `rejected_permanent`, `config`, `aborted`, `unknown` (or the refusal's reason). `classifyMailError` reads only the error's own `code` and `responseCode` data properties (no getter runs, tested), never `message`, `response`, `recipient` or `rejected`: a mail server's reply often quotes the address (tested with a 550 and a 451 that quote the address and the token).
- **The development sender** (`devTransport`): chosen by `mailerFromEnv` only when `NODE_ENV` is `development` or `test` and `OCULUS_SMTP_HOST` is not set. It logs `mail_dev` with the kind only. `OCULUS_DEV_MAIL_DIR` writes each message to a new file there (mode 0600, never overwriting) with every token replaced by `[token hidden]`; **only `OCULUS_DEV_MAIL_SHOW_TOKENS=1` shows tokens** (in those files, or on standard error without a folder). **Refused in production three ways:** `devTransport` throws when made, and its `send` rejects if the environment has turned to production since; `mailerFromEnv` refuses to start in production without SMTP, or with either development variable set. This replaces D-081's development default (which logged `reset_link_produced`, now removed). An SMTP server on localhost (Mailpit and the like) works in development through the SMTP sender.
- **Startup** (`buildAdminServer`, D-081): the mailer comes from `mailerFromEnv` unless one (or a `deliverReset`, for tests) is passed in; it also feeds T-048's `deliverInvitation` and `deliverEnrolment` hooks (D-084), replacing their development-only log, so the people routes send real mail in production instead of answering `503 delivery_unavailable`; every refusal above stops the server before it listens, the message naming the variable. SMTP needs `OCULUS_ADMIN_ORIGIN`.
- **The two-step alert notice (D-077's "required before deployment").** `sendSecondStepAlertNotices` (`packages/db/src/alert-notices.ts`) and migration **0021** (`second_step_alert_notices`; main has 0019 and T-048 takes 0020, renumber at merge if needed). Every row of `second_step_alerts` gets exactly one notice row, written once: `notified` (an email went to the person and every active system admin) or `folded`. **At most one notice per person per window** (24 hours by default; 1 hour to 7 days): a person with a `notified` row in the window has every new alert folded; otherwise the newest alert younger than the window is notified and the rest folded; alerts all older than the window (seen late, after an outage) are folded without an email, so a backlog never floods mailboxes with stale warnings.
  - **Enforced by the database, whoever writes:** one row per alert (primary key); the alert's own person; `noticed_at` now; a second `notified` row for a person within the window refused; `folded` refused unless a notice is in the window or the alert is older than it (so a writer can't silently fold a fresh alert without also writing a `notified` row); never updated (a trigger, ALWAYS, so replica mode too); the writer has INSERT only (`ALERT_NOTICE_TABLE_PRIVILEGES` in `WRITER_PRIVILEGES`), no UPDATE or DELETE. One person's rows are decided under a per-person advisory lock in the trigger (classid 85), and a whole run under `pg_advisory_xact_lock(85, 0)`, so two processes never both notify (tested racing).
  - **At most once, not at least once:** the decisions are committed before anything is handed to the mailer, so a crash or a refused send never leads to a second notice in the window. **The loss that remains:** a crash or restart after the commit and before the outbox has sent (the outbox is in memory), or a mail outage longer than the notice lane's retries (about 3 to 6 hours), loses that window's email; it is counted (`failed`) or logged by the mailer (`mail_failed`, `mail_dropped`), the next alert after the window notifies again, and **the alerts stay visible on screen** (review M2): a person's page shows system managers each recent alert (30 days, newest 10) with what became of it (`emailed`, `covered by an earlier email`, `email not sent yet`; `memberSecondStep`'s `alerts`), and the person sees a notice in the admin's frame after signing in when wrong codes were recorded for their account in the last 30 days (`GET /api/me`'s `secondStepAlerts`). *Rejected:* recording after sending (a crash between the two would mail twice, and a stuck mail server would make every run retry).
  - **Who and what.** The person (unless the account is disabled) at their own address: someone has been entering wrong two-step codes (the count and the time, in UTC), so they probably know the password; two-step sign-in kept them out; if it wasn't them, choose a new password (the only link is `/sign-in`, where "Forgot your password?" is). **Each active system admin gets one summary per run** (review M1: one email per person per admin, under credential stuffing with 12 people, gave the admin 10 emails, lost 2 for good, and then refused the admin's own reset link as `recipient_limit`), listing the people notified **by address only** (review L1: the display name is self-chosen and could put a phishing sentence in front of admins), newest first, at most 50 lines and "And N more.", leaving out the admin themselves, suggesting they confirm with each person another way and end their sessions. **A run hands over no more than the outbox's notice lane has room for** (`maxMessages`, from `outbox.room("notice")`): a person whose email and the summaries wouldn't fit is left undecided for a later run (tested). Subjects are fixed and name nobody. No token.
  - **What the guards can't stop (review L5).** The writer role can still suppress alerts: it can insert a `notified` row for an alert without sending anything, and it picks `window_seconds` (up to 7 days) for the rows it writes, so it can fold a person's alerts for up to a week behind one such row. The guards only make that visible as rows, never silent: they stop folding without a `notified` row, a second `notified` row in the window, back-dating and changes. This is about what D-077 already lets the writer do (insert fake alerts, delete counters); the on-screen trace reads `second_step_alerts` itself, which the writer can't delete from.
  - **Runs** every minute in `npm run admin` (`startAlertNotices`, `packages/api/src/alert-notices.ts`): one run at a time per process, an unref'd timer, stopped on shutdown, database failures logged by class only (`redactedError`), and `alert_notices` with the number of people notified (never who).
- **The notice after a reset (OWASP Forgot Password).** `completePasswordReset` takes `notifyChanged` (`PasswordChangedDelivery`), called after the commit, not awaited, its failures dropped; `signInRoutes` takes `deliverPasswordChanged` and the admin server passes the mailer's. The email to the account's own address says its password was changed with a reset link at a given time (UTC) and every session was signed out, and to tell the administrator at once if it wasn't them. **No token and no link but `/sign-in`** (tested end to end). It goes to the same mailbox the link went to, so it warns of a reset by someone else only where they used the link without taking the mailbox; still worth it (OWASP), and it is cheap.
- **Left for later:** notifying another address after a reset (accounts have one); DKIM signing (left to the mail service: nodemailer can sign, but the key would be one more secret); a `List-Unsubscribe` header (these are transactional messages, not bulk); an outbox that survives a restart (it would hold tokens at rest; see above).
- **Tests** (`mail.test.ts`, a fake SMTP server in the process, `smtp-server.fixtures.ts`; `mail.reset.test.ts` over a migrated database; `admin-server.test.ts`): the messages' content, the link from the configured origin only, escaping, no images or outside URLs, no token in the subject or any header, CR/LF and controls refused in the address, token, expiry and organization name (nothing reaches the server), the reply to an account and to an unknown address identical and immediate while the server is slow, only the account mailed and its link usable, the emailed set-up code accepted by the set-up route, retries with backoff then success, the cap, no retry past the link's expiry, a permanent refusal not retried, a stalled server cut off and retried, STARTTLS required, AUTH with right and wrong passwords, the per-recipient limit, a full or closed outbox, aborts, no token, address or password in any log record, the configuration's refusals (never showing the value), and the development sender refused in production and hiding tokens unless the flag is set; the alert notices (`alert-notices.test.ts`: the person and the admins told once, folding within the window and notifying again after it, late alerts folded, a disabled account and an admin about themselves, two runs racing, refused sends, every 0021 guard as the writer role and in replica mode) and the notice after a reset (its content, and end to end after a link is used); after the review: the 12-person credential-stuffing case end to end (one summary to the admin listing every address and no name, 13 emails, nothing refused, the admin's own reset link still queued), the summary's cap with "And N more", a run bounded by the outbox's room, the two lanes, notices outlasting a link's retries, nothing sent past `notAfter`, TLS failures (an untrusted certificate, a plain-text server) not retried, a trusted CA accepted, `close()`'s deadline, the person page's alert list and the frame's notice.
- Sources: RFC 5321 (SMTP: reply classes §4.2.1, 503 as a sequence error, dot-stuffing §4.5.2), RFC 5322 §2.1.1 and §2.2 (header line length, CR and LF only as line ends), RFC 3207 (STARTTLS), RFC 8314 (implicit TLS on 465 and why cleartext submission is to be avoided), RFC 2045/2046 (multipart/alternative); OWASP ASVS 5.0 V6.4 and V7.2 (as D-078) and the Forgot Password cheat sheet (tokens in links, no account details); the nodemailer 10.6.1 sources in `node_modules` (`smtp-transport` and `mailer` AbortSignal handling, `errors.js` codes, `isPermanentResponseCode`, `requireTLS`, `disableFileAccess`/`disableUrlAccess`) and its CHANGELOG (10.5.0: AbortSignal per send); MDN on URL fragments and `Referer`; read 2026-11-24.

## D-087 — System settings in the admin: site and sign-in-rule routes for system managers behind a recent sign-in, and a template editor that sends back what it doesn't show (2026-11-24, T-048a)
Builds T-048a on D-019 and D-025 (System settings are system managers'), D-072 (definitions are system managers'), D-076 (published-records confirmation), D-079 (the staff API) and D-083 (the admin's screens). Four routes in `packages/api/src/staff-api.ts`; `packages/admin/src/settings/`; a "System" group in the sidebar. No migration, no new dependency.
- **Routes.** `POST /api/sites` (`{ name, organizationId? }`, 201 `{ site }`), `PUT /api/sites/:id` (`{ name, organizationId? }`), `GET /api/system-settings` (`{ settings, version }`; an ETag from version 1) and `PUT /api/system-settings` (`{ settings }`, `If-None-Match: *` for the first change, `If-Match: "v<N>"` after). They wrap the service's `createSite`, `renameSite`, `readSystemSettings` and `changeSystemSettings`, which refuse everyone but an active system manager (`403 not_permitted`). An unknown site, a malformed site ID and a site named with the wrong organization are the same `404 not_found`. *Rejected:* a separate `/api/system/*` package (the staff API's session, CSRF, Origin and rate steps are the ones wanted); a `GET /api/sites` (`GET /api/me` already lists every site for a system manager).
- **A recent sign-in for every change** (the T-041 review: creating and renaming sites and changing System settings need re-authentication). Checked in the route from the session's `recentlyAuthenticated` (password given in the last 10 minutes, D-052), answering `403 reauthentication_required` as T-037's `endUserSessions` does. Only for a session that is a system manager's, so anyone else is told `not_permitted` by the service and never asked to sign in again for something they may not do. Reading needs no recent sign-in. The admin answers it with T-048's password-and-code re-entry form (`useReauthenticated`, D-084), shown in the page, and then makes the change. *Rejected:* the check in the service (its context carries no session); making templates need it too (T-041's review names roles, sites and System settings only).
- **The template editor edits only what it shows** — a template's name, description and record kinds; each field's key (new fields only: a saved key never changes), label, help, kind, required, repeatable, who sees it, inheritance, list choices and length limit — and **sends back everything else as it came** (standards mappings, vocabularies, identifier types, the record kinds a link may point to), dropping only the settings of a kind the field no longer has. It sends the template's `organizationId` too, so a save made after the session switched organization in another tab is refused (`wrong_organization`) instead of being stored in the other organization. **A new field is staff only** until someone chooses "Public", so adding a field never publishes anything. The server's `409 confirmation_required` is shown in the page (D-083's confirmation panel) and the template is sent again with exactly the listed `confirm` pairs, and `confirmPublished` only after the person has seen how many published records it shows values on; the server asks one thing at a time, and a later 409 keeps what was already confirmed (`confirmPublished` once given, the listed changes until the server lists new ones). A key goes into an address only once it matches core's key rule. Leaving with unsaved changes asks first (as the record editor, D-083), and the template's query never refetches on reconnect, so typed edits aren't lost to a background read. *Rejected:* a separate endpoint per field (a template is one versioned document, D-010); confirming everything up front (the person would confirm changes they hadn't been shown).
- **Who sees it.** The sidebar's "System settings" link and the `/settings` screens show only to a system manager (`systemAdmin` from `GET /api/me`); anyone else reaching the address is told the screens are for system managers, and nothing is asked of the server. The server decides regardless.
- **Left for later:** organizations with no site and no session appear only when signed in to them (there is no list of organizations yet); email, backups, updates and site passwords (T-040, T-051, T-019d).
- Sources: RFC 9110 §13.1.1–13.1.2 (`If-Match`, `If-None-Match: *`); OWASP ASVS 5.0 V7.5 (re-authentication before sensitive changes); Siteimprove Alfa 0.120's results in this run.

## D-088 — The desktop version is a launcher, not an app: the admin's own server on 127.0.0.1 in an Edge or Chrome app window, a PostgreSQL the person installs, and the secret key kept in the settings folder (2026-11-24, John and Chat D, T-056)
John asked for the desktop "extras" (an Electron window and PostgreSQL bundled inside the app).
